  ],

{
  "paper_title": "K-means-sharp: modified centroid update for outlier-robust k-means clustering",
  "authors_year": "Peter O. Olukanmi and Bhekisipho Twala, 2017",
  "research_question": "How can the classical k-means algorithm be modified to automatically detect and avoid outliers during centroid updates while preserving its basic structure, simplicity, and efficiency?",
  "context": "Real-world datasets commonly contain noise and outliers, but classical machine learning algorithms like k-means assume the absence of outliers and are easily misled by them. Existing outlier detection methods for k-means typically rely on separate pre-processing or post-processing modules, require user-supplied parameters, or introduce significant computational overhead. This paper proposes a modified clustering algorithm, k-means-sharp (k-means#), which performs clustering and automatic outlier detection simultaneously by altering only the centroid update step.",
  "methods": [
    "Modifying the classical k-means centroid update step to exclude putative outliers from cluster mean computations",
    "Deriving a global distance threshold T online from the distribution of point-to-centroid distances using the median absolute deviation (MAD)",
    "Employing 3 standard deviations as the boundary for inlier-to-centroid distance based on Gaussian data distribution assumptions",
    "Generalizing the k-means cost function to exclude outliers from contributing to the mean squared error",
    "Running comparative experiments on 10 datasets (2 standard datasets: Iris and Ruspini, and 4 outlier-contaminated versions of each with 5%, 10%, 15%, and 20% gross errors over 100 runs)"
  ],
  "main_findings": [
    "Given identical initializations, k-means# yields lower mean square error than classical k-means across all datasets tested.",
    "The outlier detection mechanism achieves an overall average accuracy of 90.4% and an overall average precision of 86.8%.",
    "The overall average recall and F1-score of the outlier detection mechanism are 52.4% and 61.1%, respectively.",
    "The algorithm eliminates the need for user intervention or prior knowledge of the number of outliers."
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "K-means assumes a Gaussian data distribution since it is an instance of the Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM).",
    "Nearly all inliers (precisely 99.73%) will have point-to-centroid distances within 3 standard deviations from the mean.",
    "The number of clusters k is known in advance."
  ],
  "limitations_explicit": [
    "The algorithm can occasionally return zero true positives due to a bad set of randomly selected initial centroids (such as when all initial centroids are outliers), inheriting this drawback from classical k-means.",
    "The cost function is non-monotonically decreasing, requiring a 'best so far' approach over a set number of iterations (e.g., 50 iterations)."
  ],

      "evidence": "The authors suggest investigating algorithm sensitivity to the number of standard deviations as a worthwhile direction for future work.",
      "evidence_excerpt": "A worthwhile direction for future work will be to investigate the sensitivity of the algorithm (in terms of each performance metric discussed) to the choice of the number of standard deviations for determining the inlier-outlier distinction threshold. Furthermore, the use of cluster-specific thresholds in contrast to a single global threshold, may improve performance, and is therefore worth exploring, although this will introduce some increase in computational cost. Lastly, since k-means# preserves the original k-means structure, it can easily benefit from many existing k-means improvements designed to address other drawbacks. The anchor-contamination results argue separately for robust estimation underneath the robust threshold: replacing the sample mean and covariance with a minimum-covariance-determinant estimator [36] would harden the calibration against dirty commissioning windows, since shrinkage [37] improves conditioning but does not, by itself, confer outlier robustness and diagonal, block-diagonal or low-rank covariance summaries could additionally cut communication. A physical deployment pass also remains to be done: on-device timing, memory and measured (rather than estimated) energy on microcontroller hardware, ideally together with a live pilot. Evaluation on further device-centric corpora such as Bot-IoT is a separate open item. Over a longer horizon, AF-BKM could be combined with the earlier ensemble idea, providing a stable, calibrated first-stage BKM signal while lightweight local detectors are invoked only near ambiguous decision regions, which the evasion analysis suggests is where they are most needed.",
      "sections": [
        "7.4. Future Work",
        "Page 22"
      ]
